Abstract On-Policy Mechanism Distillation improves language model distillation by transferring teacher intermediate computations compatible with the student's architecture, yielding stronger reasoning and internalization.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small On-policy distillation (OPD) improves language-model by supervising a student on prefixes sampled from its own policy, but its supervision remains confined to the output space. Consequently, OPD transfers what a teacher predicts without supervising the intermediate computation that produces those predictions. Simply distilling intermediate representations like representation matching is insufficient since teacher and student representations may differ geometrically, and even well-aligned hidden states need not be usable by the student's subsequent computation. We introduce On-Policy Mechanism Distillation (OPMD), which transfers teacher-derived intermediate computation while explicitly accounting for its compatibility with the student. OPMD first constructs a student-compatible teacher that transforms teacher representations into intermediate states usable by the student's downstream computation, then it internalizes these states from on-policy trajectories. OPMD achieves the highest average accuracy among the evaluated distillation baselines in all three teacher--student settings, and generalizes to multi-teacher distillation and cross-family supervised fine-tuning. Further analyses show that mechanism supervision yields more effective reasoning and meaningfully alters the student's intermediate computation and solution strategies, supporting that it transfers information beyond output supervision alone. Together, these results establish mechanism-level supervision as an effective way to move on-policy distillation beyond output supervision, enabling students to learn inner mechanisms they can use and internalize.
[19.10.2026 08:27] Response: {"desc": "Статья предлагает метод On-Policy Mechanism Distillation (OPMD), который улучшает дистилляцию языковых моделей. Вместо того чтобы учить студента только на выходных предсказаниях учителя, OPMD переносит промежуточные вычисления (скрытые представления) учителя. Перед передачей учительские представления преобразуются в student-compatible формат, чтобы студент мог использовать их в своих последующих слоях. Эксперименты показывают лучшую точность по сравнению с базовыми методами дистилляции, улучшенные рассуждения и обобщение на multi-teacher дистилляцию и кросс-семейный SFT.", "emoji": "🧠", "title": "Учим студента не только ответам, но и тому, как думать"}

"Abstract On-Policy Mechanism Distillation improves language model distillation by transferring teacher intermediate computations compatible with the student's architecture, yielding stronger reasoning and internalization.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small On-policy distillation (OPD) improves language-model by supervising a student on prefixes sampled from its own policy, but its supervision remains confined to the output space. Consequently, OPD transfers what a teacher predicts without supervising the intermediate computation that produces those predictions. Simply distilling intermediate representations like representation matching is insufficient since teacher and student representations may differ geometrically, and even well-aligned hidden states need not be usable by the student's subsequent computation. We introduce On-Policy Mechanism Distillation (OPMD), which transfers teacher-derived intermediate computation while explicitly accounting for its compatibility with the student. OPMD first constructs a student-compatible teacher that transforms teacher representations into intermediate states usable by the student's downstream computation, then it internalizes these states from on-policy trajectories. OPMD achieves the highest average accuracy among the evaluated distillation baselines in all three teacher--student settings, and generalizes to multi-teacher distillation and cross-family supervised fine-tuning. Further analyses show that mechanism supervision yields more effective reasoning and meaningfully alters the student's intermediate computation and solution strategies, supporting that it transfers information beyond output supervision alone. Together, these results establish mechanism-level supervision as an effective way to move on-policy distillation beyond output supervision, enabling students to learn inner mechanisms they can use and internalize."

Abstract PremiseCheck improves peer review verification by decomposing comments into explicit premises checked by decision models, achieving high accuracy with lower cost and latency.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Peer review is under strain from the growth of submissions, and review comments written by humans or AI can misstate what a paper says. We introduce ReviewFact, a benchmark that labels such factual errors in review comments under one definition across NLP, machine learning, and the natural sciences, and that measures cost and latency alongside accuracy. A frontier LLM checks comments well but must read the whole paper for each check, while cheap decision models, which score whether a statement holds in a text, fall far short on whole comments that mix several implicit assertions. PremiseCheck is a framework that combines the two. An LLM rewrites each comment as explicit premises without reading the paper, and a decision model checks each premise against the paper. When each comment is checked on its own, PremiseCheck reaches accuracy comparable to GPT-5.5 across all three fields at about half its cost, and on NLP reviews at a quarter of its cost and less than half its latency.

Abstract Isolating recurrence design axes reveals that a prelude-recurrent block-coda sandwich architecture improves reasoning and context extraction, with advantages transferring to parameter-efficient Mixture-of-Experts models.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Looped language models reuse physical Transformer blocks across logical depth, but established architectures entangle recurrence placement, input injection, and recurrent state organization. We isolate these axes in models trained from scratch at matched parameter scale, logical depth, and token budgets, and then test whether the resulting design principles transfer to resident- and active-parameter-matched Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models. The prelude-recurrent block-coda sandwich architecture consistently improves performance on context extraction and reasoning-intensive tasks. Input injection primarily enhances knowledge retrieval and context utilization, whereas random initialization and separate H/L recurrent states provide mixed or negative returns. Taken together, these results favor the Huginn-style design: at the 730M-parameter and 336B-token scale, the complete Huginn architecture outperforms Ouro across all ten benchmarks. The advantages of Huginn over Ouro transfer to MoE models. At the 8BA0.8B (32BA3B when unrolled) scale trained on 500B tokens, Huginn outperforms Ouro on nearly all benchmarks spanning knowledge, reasoning, and coding, while also exhibiting more balanced expert routing. Under matched training and inference FLOPs, the Huginn-style model uses 75% fewer resident parameters than a 112-layer feedforward MoE baseline. Despite its smaller physical parameters, it outperforms the feedforward baseline on DROP and GSM8K, matches it on MATH500, and remains competitive on the other benchmarks.

Авторы изолируют ключевые оси проектирования: размещение рекуррентности, инъекцию входа (input injection) и организацию рекуррентного состояния, обучая модели с нуля при равных параметрах, глубине и объёме токенов. Abstract BELIEFWEAVER improves long-horizon agent recovery by combining persistent evidence with prospective reasoning and distilling reasoning into a smaller model.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Long-horizon agents often struggle to recover from failures whose causes lie several steps earlier, beyond what local reflection or backtracking can resolve. We introduce BELIEFWEAVER, a framework that combines persistent evidence across attempts with prospective reasoning over repair directions. It maintains a relational belief state over frontiers, attempts, observations, and checkpoints. Execution evidence updates related frontier priorities across attempts and rollback, while diagnostic evidence gates execution. An implicit world model (IWM) evaluates repair hypotheses, predicts progress, and identifies relations among frontiers to guide exploration. We further distill these recurring IWM reasoning components into a 9B student through supervised fine-tuning and on-policy distillation (OPD). Across three terminal-based benchmarks, BELIEFWEAVER improves aggregate final score by 5.1 percentage points over native Terminus-2 continuation, with positive trends in exploration efficiency. The distilled 9B student improves final score by 9.8 percentage points over the reported native baseline while reducing estimated teacher API cost by 54.8% relative to the matched teacher. These results show that persistent evidence and prospective reasoning can improve recovery in long-horizon agent tasks while substantially reducing reliance on teacher-model inference.

Abstract FC-SWE improves reinforcement learning for software engineering agents by conditioning recovery attempts on failed patch feedback, boosting resolution rates across multiple attempts.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Repository-level software engineering (SWE) is a challenging long-horizon setting: agents must reason over extended interactions, use tools, and adapt to stateful environments. Recent work trains SWE agents with reinforcement learning methods such as Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), which independently sample multiple trajectories per issue, test the resulting patches, and compare terminal rewards within a fixed group. However, this training setup does not reuse verifier feedback from failed patches as context for subsequent attempts, even though this feedback contains valuable diagnostic information about what went wrong. Training on recovery trajectories is challenging because the preceding outcome determines whether the next trajectory is generated, while the failed execution determines its conditioning context. We introduce FC-SWE, a failure-conditioned RL framework that incorporates recovery attempts into policy training. After a patch fails verification, FC-SWE restores the repository to its original task state and uses the failed patch and verifier feedback as context for a recovery trajectory. FC-SWE adapts GRPO to these chains of complete, multi-turn tool-use trajectories through two mechanisms. Trajectory-local rewards preserve each attempt's verifier outcome, preventing recovery success from rewarding an earlier failed patch. Active-set advantage estimation forms a comparison group from all initial and recovery trajectories actually executed for the same issue, so failed attempts remain in the group while unexecuted attempts are excluded. On all 500 SWE-bench Verified tasks under a verifier-assisted protocol, FC-SWE with Qwen3.5-4B and SWE-agent achieves 41.7% Resolved@1 and 52.8% Resolved@2, compared with 38.9% and 48.5% for GRPO. Although trained with at most two attempts per chain, FC-SWE reaches 70.7% Resolved@11 under an eleven-attempt test-time budget.
[19.10.2026 08:27] Response: {"desc": "Статья предлагает FC-SWE, фреймворк обучения с подкреплением для агентов, решающих задачи уровня репозитория в разработке ПО. После неудачного патча среда возвращается к исходному состоянию, а неудачный патч и обратная связь верификатора передаются как контекст для попытки восстановления. Метод адаптирует GRPO через локальные для траектории награды и оценку преимуществ по активному набору, что учитывает все реально выполненные попытки. На SWE-bench Verified модель Qwen3.5-4B показывает Resolved@1 41.7% и Resolved@2 52.8%, превосходя GRPO (38.9% и 48.5%). Один и тот же агент с бюджетом из одиннадцати попыток достигает 70.7% Resolved@11.", "emoji": "🛠️", "title": "Учимся на ошибках: обратная связь от неудачных патчей улучшает обучение SWE-агентов"}

Abstract CQ2-CARE improves software issue resolution by selecting exact files to include verbatim within a token budget, outperforming compression and retrieval methods.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small A coding agent works on repositories that can be far larger than its context window can hold. Prompt compressors and retrievers offer solutions to this problem, but patching an issue needs two things at once: the files that must change, and their code exactly as written, since the model must reproduce the lines it edits. Retrievers rank code by its similarity to the issue rather than by whether it must change, and compressors delete text to fit the budget. We show that at an equal token budget, an agent resolves more issues when the budget goes to choosing which files to show verbatim, as our simple selector CQ2-CARE does, than when it goes to pruning tokens or retrieving chunks. We fit every context to the same per-issue budget on 300 recent SWE-rebench issues and grade each patch with the benchmark's tests. With GPT-5.6 and Claude Opus 5, CQ2-CARE resolves 43.7% and 46.7% of the issues, 6.3 points more than the best of three compressors with either model, and 9.3 and 12.7 points more than the best of three retrievers. CQ2-CARE even resolves more issues than two methods trained for issue resolution, SweRank (by 0.7 and 1.7 points) and SWE-Pruner (by 2.0 and 7.3). Our analysis shows the defects of the baseline methods: retrievers often miss the files to edit, and compressors keep them but alter their text, so fewer patches apply. Exact-file selection needs no compression model and shows every line as the repository has it, so the model can quote the code it edits.

Авторы показывают, что при одинаковом бюджете токенов лучше выбирать файлы, которые нужно изменить, и передавать их verbatim, чем сжимать текст или использовать retrieval. Метод CQ2-CARE без compression model превосходит компрессоры, retriever-методы и даже SweRank и SWE-Pruner на SWE-rebench. Abstract LEV is a looped decision model that increases reasoning depth by iterating its layers without generating tokens, improving accuracy on hard questions while allowing adaptive computation via a stopper.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Decision models answer typed questions about an input with one probability per option, computed in a single forward pass without generating text. Like the fast System 1 of dual-process theory, they answer at once, but their depth is fixed at training time, so they spend the same computation on easy and hard questions. Models that reason in generated text can think longer, but they pay for every extra step in decoded tokens. We present LEV, a decision model that thinks deeper by rerunning its layers in a loop instead of generating tokens. LEV builds on a language model pretrained as a loop, reads the options after every loop step and trains all steps jointly, so one checkpoint can answer at any depth. On three looped backbones, accuracy rises with depth on the same weights, mostly on hard questions, and a 2.6B LEV goes from 63.2% at depth one to 76.2% at depth four on JevBench. On-demand depth, set per question by a small stopper with a threshold fitted on unlabelled requests, saves 15% to 24% of the computation at a cost of under one accuracy point, level with or slightly above a fixed mix of depths. On a locked test set, the 1.4B and 3B models are 5.4 to 8.8 points more accurate than dense (non-looped) models and cascades of the same latency below 50 ms.

Модель построена на языковой модели, предобученной как loop-архитектура, и обучается совместно на всех шагах цикла, поэтому один чекпоинт может отвечать с разной глубиной. Abstract A training-free weight-editing method reduces instruction-tuned model compliance with harmful behaviors by applying contrast-based edits to model weights using minimal prompt pairs.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Instruction-following language models can comply with requests that conflict with intended behaviors and safety alignment. We introduce a training-free weight-editing approach for reducing compliance with targeted undesirable instructions. Our method needs only forward passes on a small set of prompt pairs per target behavior, and because the edit is stored in the model's weights, changing or deleting instructions in the prompt cannot undo the weight modification. We evaluate the approach on two target harmful behaviors across five instruction-tuned models: instructed falsehood and instructed sexism. On two instructed-lying benchmarks, it improves mean honest response rates by 6.2 and 4.6 percentage points over the strongest alternative, significantly on three of the five models and within noise on the other two. In a controlled study of seven prompt formats, it maintains higher honest-response rates than single-layer activation steering when orders to lie arrive in single-turn formats not used to construct the edit. For instructed sexism, it reduces the mean sexist-response rate from 57.9% to 24.3%; combining the edit with a fairness prompt reduces it further to 11.9%. These results establish our contrast-based weight editing approach as an effective training-free intervention in two distinct behavioral domains, supporting its use both independently and alongside prompt-based safeguards.

Для каждого целевого поведения используется лишь несколько пар промптов и только forward pass, а контрастная правка сохраняется в весах модели. Поскольку изменение зашито в веса, его нельзя отменить изменением промпта. Abstract DAGFormer improves Transformer efficiency by using a jointly trained topology predictor to plan cross-layer connections globally, reducing perplexity and boosting downstream task performance across model scales.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small We propose DAGFormer, a Transformer language model whose cross-layer connections are planned by a jointly trained topology predictor. Beyond the residual connections in a standard Transformer, DAGFormer adds direct connections from earlier layers to later ones, and the predictor controls the strength of every connection based on input tokens. Unlike existing cross-layer routing architectures, which set each layer's connections separately, DAGFormer plans the routing of the whole model at once. On matched OLMo-2 pairs from 75M to 1B parameters, DAGFormer lowers WikiText-2 perplexity by 17% to 25% over the vanilla Transformer, and a 1B DAGFormer trained on 5B tokens outperforms the vanilla model trained on 10B. Against four routing baselines with the same data and budget, it reaches the lowest perplexity at 75M, 300M and 1B. The gain carries over to 13 commonsense, science, reading and math evaluation tasks (2.0 to 2.5 points higher mean accuracy at 300M and 1B) and to instruction finetuning (63% to 70% AlpacaEval win rate against the vanilla Transformer, leading every routing baseline), and under structured pruning DAGFormer degrades the least in five of six settings.
[19.10.2026 08:28] Response: {"desc": "Авторы предлагают DAGFormer — Transformer, в котором помимо обычных residual connections добавлены прямые связи между более ранними и поздними слоями. Силу каждой связи регулирует отдельный topology predictor, который обучается совместно с моделью и учитывает входные токены. В отличие от аналогов, маршрутизация строится для всей сети сразу, а не для каждого слоя отдельно. Эксперименты показывают снижение perplexity на WikiText-2 на 17–25%, рост качества на downstream-задачах и instruction finetuning, а также лучшую устойчивость к structured pruning.", "emoji": "🧭", "title": "Планируем маршруты всей сети разом — и Transformer становится эффективнее"}

Abstract EvoHeir improves self-evolving language model loops by separating parameter and experience inheritance and using fresh evaluation to confirm updates, reducing harmful inheritance and boosting reasoning performance.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Self-evolving language models train on tasks they generate, so every round must decide which updated model the next round inherits. Current loops such as R-Zero, Agent0, and Skill Self-Play make this decision through the task, admitting tasks that look learnable and inheriting every resulting update. We identify a selection-to-update gap in these loops, where a task that looks learnable need not produce a useful update. Most updates on verified tasks fail to make correct answers more likely, yet these loops inherit them all. HeirCommit freezes the updated model and uses fresh evaluation questions to decide whether to inherit it or retain the current model. EvoHeir separates parameter inheritance from experience inheritance, using current and archived experience to repair failures and preserve successes before final confirmation. Our analysis characterizes selection-induced optimism and decomposes regret into selection, absorption, and confirmation errors. HeirCommit cuts the harmful share of accepted updates by 68-83% relative to random inheritance at the same acceptance rate. Across four backbones, EvoHeir achieves the best mathematics and general-reasoning averages, raising the math average by 5.3% relative to the strongest baseline, Agent0. The framework lets useful experience persist even when the model that produced it is not inherited.
[19.10.2026 08:28] Response: {"desc": "В статье предлагается EvoHeir — фреймворк для самоэволюционирующих языковых моделей (LLM), которые обучаются на задачах, сгенерированных ими же. Авторы выявляют разрыв между отбором задач и качеством обновлений: многие обновления не делают правильные ответы более вероятными, но всё равно наследуются. Метод HeirCommit замораживает обновлённую модель и проверяет её на свежих оценочных вопросах, прежде чем решить, наследовать ли её. EvoHeir разделяет наследование параметров и наследование опыта, что позволяет сохранять полезный опыт даже без наследования породившей его модели, и на тестах повышает качество математических и общих reasoning-способностей.", "emoji": "🧬", "title": "Наследуй только то, что реально улучшает модель"}

"Abstract EvoHeir improves self-evolving language model loops by separating parameter and experience inheritance and using fresh evaluation to confirm updates, reducing harmful inheritance and boosting reasoning performance.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Self-evolving language models train on tasks they generate, so every round must decide which updated model the next round inherits. Current loops such as R-Zero, Agent0, and Skill Self-Play make this decision through the task, admitting tasks that look learnable and inheriting every resulting update. We identify a selection-to-update gap in these loops, where a task that looks learnable need not produce a useful update. Most updates on verified tasks fail to make correct answers more likely, yet these loops inherit them all. HeirCommit freezes the updated model and uses fresh evaluation questions to decide whether to inherit it or retain the current model. EvoHeir separates parameter inheritance from experience inheritance, using current and archived experience to repair failures and preserve successes before final confirmation. Our analysis characterizes selection-induced optimism and decomposes regret into selection, absorption, and confirmation errors. HeirCommit cuts the harmful share of accepted updates by 68-83% relative to random inheritance at the same acceptance rate. Across four backbones, EvoHeir achieves the best mathematics and general-reasoning averages, raising the math average by 5.3% relative to the strongest baseline, Agent0. The framework lets useful experience persist even when the model that produced it is not inherited."

Abstract ReaDy accelerates autoregressive generation by using hybrid linear attention recurrent states to retrieve position-specific context for speculative drafting, improving speed and acceptance rates.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Speculative decoding accelerates autoregressive generation by drafting multiple tokens per step, yet feature-based drafters rely on target hidden features whose contextual emphasis may not align with the needs of subsequent draft positions. Hybrid linear attention (HLA) models maintain compact recurrent states that accumulate historical associations, offering an efficient source of complementary context for speculative drafting. We introduce ReaDy, the first speculative decoding method, to the best of our knowledge, to leverage HLA recurrent states for position-specific context retrieval during drafting. ReaDy equips each draft position with a learned query to retrieve supplementary context directly from the target's committed recurrent states. A lightweight state reader integrates this context into draft representations through residual updates, while low-rank state reads and fused operators keep additional computation and I/O overhead low. Building on DSpark's parallel backbone and sequential prediction head, ReaDy employs a lightweight two-stage adaptation strategy, with reader capacity, state sources, and residual placement examined through ablation studies. Across seven benchmarks spanning mathematics, code generation, and dialogue, ReaDy achieves an average 3.16times decoding speedup over autoregressive decoding with Qwen3.8-27B on a single NVIDIA B200 GPU. It outperforms DSpark on every benchmark, improving throughput by 7.97% and acceptance length by 8.50% on average. Our code is available at https://github.com/StartLuxLabs/ReaDy.

Для каждой позиции черновика обучаемый запрос извлекает дополнительный контекст из рекуррентных состояний hybrid linear attention (HLA) целевой модели. Лёгкий state reader встраивает этот контекст в представления драфтера через residual-обновления, а low-rank чтения состояний снижают накладные расходы. На семи бенчмарках метод даёт в среднем ускорение декодирования в 3.16 раза и опережает DSpark по пропускной способности и длине принятых токенов.", "emoji": "🚀", "title": "Память рекуррентных состояний ускоряет генерацию текста"}

Abstract Dynamic Gaussian representations are improved by allocating primitives based on predicted spatiotemporal destinations rather than current locations, recovering small fast-moving objects and fine textures.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Dynamic Gaussian representations have made high-quality, real-time volumetric video increasingly practical, yet challenging motion can still break their reconstruction: small fast-moving objects may disappear, and fine textures in highly dynamic regions are often lost. In particular, simply training longer or using more Gaussians may still fail to recover small, fast-moving objects. We argue that the key bottleneck is not representational capacity itself, but where and when Gaussian primitives are allocated. Existing allocation strategies rely on current Gaussian locations and often overlook the spatiotemporal destinations of relocated Gaussians. To address these limitations, we introduce two complementary strategies. First, Seed Injection identifies under-reconstructed dynamic regions using temporal appearance changes and reconstruction errors, and initializes new Gaussians at geometrically plausible 3D locations through multi-view observations, independent of existing Gaussian locations. Second, Temporal Biased Relocation uses time-resolved optimization signals to guide the spatiotemporal placement of relocated Gaussians. Both strategies maintain a fixed Gaussian budget without modifying the underlying representation. Experiments across multiple dynamic-scene datasets demonstrate improved reconstruction quality, particularly in highly dynamic regions, recovering small, rapidly moving objects and fine-grained appearance details.
[19.10.2026 08:28] Response: {"desc": "Статья посвящена улучшению динамических Gaussian-представлений для объемного видео в реальном времени, которые плохо восстанавливают маленькие быстро движущиеся объекты и мелкие текстуры. Авторы утверждают, что ключевая проблема — не ёмкость представления, а то, где и когда размещаются Gaussian-примитивы, поскольку существующие стратегии аллокации опираются на текущие позиции и не учитывают пространственно-временные места назначения. Предложены два дополняющих метода: Seed Injection, которая создаёт новые Gaussian-примитивы в геометрически правдоподобных 3D-точках по многовидовым наблюдениям, и Temporal Biased Relocation, которая использует временные сигналы оптимизации для направленного перемещения примитивов. Оба подхода сохраняют фиксированный бюджет Gaussian-примитивов без изменения базового представления и показывают улучшение качества реконструкции в динамичных областях на нескольких датасетах.", "emoji": "🎬", "title": "Важно не сколько Gaussian-примитивов, а где и когда их разместить"}

Second, Temporal Biased Relocation uses time-resolved optimization signals to guide the spatiotemporal placement of relocated Gaussians. Both strategies maintain a fixed Gaussian budget without modifying the underlying representation. Experiments across multiple dynamic-scene datasets demonstrate improved reconstruction quality, particularly in highly dynamic regions, recovering small, rapidly moving objects and fine-grained appearance details."

Abstract SAE-IDF matches symbolic units across heterogeneous systems using sparse autoencoders and rarity-weighted features, enabling scalable neuro-symbolic reasoning.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Large language models (LLMs) hold broad knowledge in one set of weights, but lack interpretability and trustworthy reasoning, especially in high-stakes use cases. A neuro-symbolic model, with its interpretable architecture and System 2 reasoning capacity, is a promising alternative if built at the scale of LLMs; however, its symbolic backbone is usually constructed with human effort, so existing neuro-symbolic models are bottlenecked by the scope of the symbol component and usually domain-specific. A data-driven method could instead build a larger symbolic system by aligning and merging existing ones. We target its first, foundational step: matching units across these heterogeneous systems. Our method, SAE-IDF, encodes a symbol in its native context into a sparse code with a sparse autoencoder (SAE), re-weights each dimension by its rarity and matches across systems. On 12 tasks across 6 kinds of symbolic system, SAE-IDF outperforms the same LLM's dense baseline on all tasks with Gemma-2-9B and all but one with Llama-3.1-8B, by up to 45 points, consistently across SAE layers and configurations. SAE-IDF is also interpretable: every dimension of the sparse code has a human-readable meaning, so each match decomposes into named concepts (features). Our work paves the way to a universal symbolic backbone.

Каждый символ кодируется в свой исходный контекст с помощью разреженного автоэнкодера (SAE), после чего каждое измерение разреженного кода взвешивается по редкости. Abstract DuET is a dual-encoder transformer that jointly performs MIDI-to-score transcription and beat tracking to improve timing and duration accuracy in piano performance transcription.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Transcribing expressive piano performances into scores requires interpreting individual note timings within an underlying metrical structure. However, score attributes are associated with notes, whereas beats may occur without coincident note onsets. We introduce DuET, a Dual-Encoder Transformer that jointly learns note-level performance MIDI-to-score (PM2S) transcription and frame-level beat and downbeat tracking. Bidirectional cross-attention connects note and frame encoders, while an autoregressive decoder alternates between their representations to predict structured score attributes. We further introduce TimeRoPE and Time-Pitch Rotary Positional Encoding (TPRoPE) to establish a shared performance-time reference and explicitly encode relative temporal and pitch relationships. Evaluations on ASAP, a classical piano dataset, and our curated Gangqinpu dataset, which spans multiple genres with a focus on popular piano music, demonstrate improvements in score timing and notated duration. Compared with the strongest baseline on each dataset, DuET reduces MUSTER mean error from 6.56% to 3.64% on Gangqinpu and from 11.30% to 11.22% on ASAP. Ablations support the benefits of frame-level context, time-based positional encoding, and decoder-history masking. Code, weights, and demos are available at https://github.com/wei-zeng98/DuET.
[19.10.2026 08:28] Response: {"desc": "DuET — это dual-encoder transformer, который одновременно решает две задачи: транскрипцию MIDI в нотную запись (PM2S) на уровне нот и отслеживание битов и долей на уровне фреймов. Два энкодера связаны двунаправленным cross-attention, а autoregressive decoder по очереди опирается на их представления, предсказывая структурированные атрибуты нот. Авторы вводят TimeRoPE и TPRoPE, чтобы задать общую временную шкалу исполнения и явно кодировать относительные временные и тональные связи. Prompt: Read an abstract of the ML paper and return a JSON with fields: 'desc': explanation of the paper in Russian (4 sentences), use correct machine learning terms. 'emoji': emoji that will reflect the theme of an article somehow, only one emoji. 'title': a slogan of a main idea of the article in Russian. Return only JSON and nothing else.

Abstract Consistent Distribution Matching accelerates diffusion and flow models via simulation-free, data-free distillation that unifies generation and score estimation, achieving strong one-step and few-step results.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Flow and diffusion models suffer from slow inference due to computationally expensive numerical integration. Distillation provides a promising way for a student model to learn from a teacher's dynamics, enabling one-step or few-step generation. However, existing methods often depend on curated distillation datasets, costly teacher rollouts, or auxiliary proxy networks, which complicate model training and scaling. In this work, we propose Consistent Distribution Matching, a simulation-free and data-free distillation method for accelerating diffusion and flow models while preserving strong generative capacity. Our key insight is to unify sample generation and score estimation with one student network. Thus, our framework uses only two models, a frozen teacher and a trainable student, and optimizes one objective. We prove that minimizing our objective indicates Wasserstein convergence of the student flow-map pushforwards to the teacher marginals. On ImageNet 256times256, our method attains an FID of 2.04 with a single function evaluation (1-NFE) and a 4-NFE FID of 1.37 within 40 epochs of training, surpassing the state-of-the-art distillation baselines without data. Our code code and model are available at https://consistentdmd.github.io/.

Ключевая идея в том, что одна student-сеть одновременно генерирует сэмплы и оценивает score, поэтому используется всего две модели (замороженный teacher и обучаемый student) и одна целевая функция. Авторы доказывают, что минимизация этой функции ведёт к сходимости по Вассерштейну pushforward-распределений flow-map student-модели к маргиналам teacher. Abstract GeoState introduces a view-independent geometric representation that separates scene dynamics from camera motion for predictive 3D world modeling, enabling consistent multi-view forecasting and cross-dataset generalization.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Recent 3D world models increasingly use features from geometry foundation models (GFMs) as predictive world states, benefiting from their rich geometric priors. However, the view dependence of these features entangles scene dynamics with camera-induced variation, making the resulting state evolution harder to predict. We propose GeoState, a view-decoupled geometry representation for predictive 3D world modeling that separates scene-state evolution from camera-dependent observation. Specifically, a scene tokenizer aggregates multi-view GFM features into a view-decoupled scene state, which a geometry decoder queries using camera rays. An autoregressive world model then predicts future scene states, while camera motion is modeled separately. The predicted states can therefore be decoded under either predicted or externally specified cameras, while retaining a single context-derived scale throughout the rollout without per-frame scale alignment. Experiments show that GeoState outperforms prior 3D world models on Waymo, supports geometrically consistent cross-view querying, and generalizes zero-shot to KITTI and nuScenes. Project page: https://hongchang-liu.github.io/GeoState-project-page/
[19.10.2026 08:29] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет GeoState — представление геометрии сцены, не зависящее от точки обзора камеры, которое отделяет динамику сцены от движения камеры. Scene tokenizer агрегирует признаки geometry foundation models с нескольких видов в view-decoupled состояние сцены, а geometry decoder извлекает геометрию по лучам камеры. Autoregressive world model предсказывает будущие состояния сцены, а движение камеры моделируется отдельно, что обеспечивает единый масштаб на всём rollout без выравнивания по кадрам. Эксперименты показывают превосходство над предыдущими 3D world models на Waymo и zero-shot обобщение на KITTI и nuScenes.", "emoji": "🌍", "title": "Отделяем динамику мира от взгляда камеры для согласованного 3D-прогнозирования"}
